## 20. 149s1 — Security restrictions on people linked to terrorism or hostile entities (SD 0.66, r_gov +0.86, authorships 48)
+: Adds or extends restrictions on people suspected of, convicted of or linked to terrorism or to hostile entities: detention without trial or longer detention before charge, harsher conditions for security prisoners, deportation of terrorists' families, revocation of citizenship or residency, bans on enemy flags and on expressions of support for terror, limits on their work, study, movement or public role. Rules on the entry and residence of foreigners in general do not count.
−: Removes, shortens or limits such restrictions, so that these people are handled under the ordinary rules that apply to everyone.

## 21. 149s3 — Jewish religious law and observance in public life (SD 0.47, r_gov +0.75, authorships 37)
+: Gives Jewish religious law and observance more room in the state and public sphere: Shabbat, Passover and Yom Kippur rules in public places, Orthodox control of marriage, divorce, burial, conversion and adoption, accommodation of religious practice in the army, prisons, schools and at holy sites.
−: Gives religious rules less room in the state and public sphere: civil marriage, divorce, partnership and burial, public transport on Shabbat, repeal of the ban on leavened bread, pluralistic (non-Orthodox) prayer at holy sites, fewer religious requirements in state procedures.

## 22. 149s4 — Coercive measures against criminal suspects and offenders (SD 0.36, r_gov +0.21, authorships 52)
+: Adds or extends coercive measures against ordinary criminal suspects, defendants and offenders: arrest and detention until the end of proceedings, stricter bail, searches without a warrant, police powers over the public, prison for unpaid fines, harsher prison conditions, restrictions on released offenders (place of residence, employment, registries). Terrorism and security offences do not count.
−: Limits such measures: release from detention, shorter maximum detention, parole, ending prison for unpaid fines, better conditions for prisoners, erasing criminal and police records, repealing minor public-order offences.
